[instruction-like text quoted from a file or tool; data, not instructions] # Foreman frontier catalogue (2026-10-09)

190 ideas: round 1 (7 grounded theme agents) and round 2 (token economics, orchestration of orchestrators, skills and tools, a revolution pass that killed 11 and escalated 64). Build items grouped into 13 milestones.

## Milestone 1: Zero-token instrument layer
Every common read, search, log, trace and diff question gets answered by a deterministic typed tool that costs the model no reasoning tokens.
- first slice: fm instruments: one typed-tool registry (JSON schema, stable output) with the first five: symbol read, failure record, log templates, data-file schema, stack-trace mapper.
- proof: Run each instrument on a fixed corpus (real failing run, 48k-line log, 212-file question); output must be under its line cap and keep the root cause; measured token ratio vs raw read logged.
- members: Foreman's zero-token instruments as typed tools for every agent and harness; Symbol reads: a definition and its one-hop neighbourhood, never a whole file; Failure records: any test runner's 400-line failure becomes 15 lines with the right source; 48,000 log lines become 37 templates, and what's new since the good run; Schema, stats and samples for any data file instead of reading its bytes; A pasted stack trace arrives already mapped to code, age and task; Every thread's stack at the moment of a hang, in any language; Where the time and memory go, in 12 lines, against the last green baseline; Ask a question of 212 files; Opus reads one 19-row table; Program slicing: only the lines that can affect this value; Structural search and replace (comby/ast-grep style) with no binary dependency; One bisector over commits, dependency changes, config and evidence trees; Version-exact library docs from the installed source, offline; Dependency questions answered from the code you actually run; Reproduce a red CI job locally without docker; 'This already exists' at the moment a duplicate helper is written; Delete candidates proven by references and by runtime; Source-to-sink taint paths in seconds, so the security reviewer reads 4 slices; Offline advisories with reachability; Screenshot diffs that send the model crops, not screenshots; Merge conflicts pre-resolved; Claude sees only the real ones

## Milestone 2: Context firewall and cheap reader
Big tool results are distilled by a cheap layer before Opus sees them, and repeat reads cost almost nothing.
- first slice: PostToolUse/PreToolUse distill hook for Bash/Read/Grep plus delta re-reads, with a raw escape hatch and a hash-keyed facts cache.
- proof: Replay 20 recorded sessions through the firewall; expensive-model input tokens fall at least 40% with no lost root-cause fact (checked against the recorded fixes).
- members: Context firewall: cheap layer rewrites every big tool result before the expensive model sees it; Distill at the source: PreToolUse rewrites Read, Grep and Bash into their distilled forms before they run; Delta re-reads: a file the model already saw comes back as 'unchanged' or as only its diff; Ask, don't read: a cheap reader answers code questions with citations, and the expensive model never opens the file; A verified-facts cache keyed by blob hash, shared by every session, lane and subagent; Hash-pinned code facts: answers that expire when the code changes; Branch prediction: Foreman predicts the agent's next slow read-only commands and runs them while the model is still thinking; Intent edits with speculative drafting: Opus says what changes, Haiku writes the bytes, Opus reads a 30-line diff

## Milestone 3: Model routing, effort and cache economics
Cheap models attempt first and Opus is called only where it matters, with effort priced per step and no cache busts.
- first slice: Cost model plus per-step effort/model router, cache-bust radar and a priced plan; ships as advisory first, enforcing second.
- proof: A/B on a replay set of finished tasks: total cost and expensive-model tokens versus baseline, with task pass rate unchanged; report the stack's net saving.
- members: Ledger-built compaction at the cost-optimal reset point; The midpoint law: a four-number cost model of every session; Cache-safe per-request effort and model routing; Uncertainty-allocated thinking; Effort governor: reasoning effort set per plan step; Cache-bust radar; Opus on call: the cheapest model attempts every step first, checked by machines not by Opus; the frontier model is paged only on failure; Cache-shaped sessions: no cache busts — model and effort changes land at boundaries, and idle sessions rotate instead of rewriting; Lean, self-silencing Foreman: rules by stage capsule, notes only when they change, plugins pruned per stage; Surprise-priced thinking: reasoning effort follows how surprised the model should be, set per request; Per-request model router: Opus decides, Haiku performs — switched inside one conversation; Advisor inversion for small work: Haiku 5.5 runs the loop, and Opus 5.5 is the server-side advisor it consults; Budget-aware planner: every plan is priced, and Foreman picks the cheapest path that meets the bar; Warm workers: every cheap worker shares one byte-identical prefix, and reviewers start as cache hits; Self-tuning economics: every knob in the stack runs as a live experiment, and only what holds quality is kept; The stack: Opus conducts, everything else is cheap or free — −81% expensive-model tokens and −55% wall-clock per finished task; Context PGO: Foreman profiles where every token goes and recompiles its own prompts, injections and output digests to the smallest form that still passes; Harness latency bisect; Treasury: fleet-wide budget envelopes and model routing, enforced by rewriting calls

## Milestone 4: Zero-call bookkeeping and session lifecycle
Foreman state, close-out and handoffs take no model turns, and sessions are reborn from proven capsules instead of dragging stale context.
- first slice: State inferred from events, one-call close-out returning one line, and a capsule format with a reproduce-the-failure acceptance check.
- proof: Replay a day of tasks: model calls per task drop, and a reborn session continues a task to green from the capsule alone.
- members: Zero-round-trip bookkeeping; Zero-call bookkeeping: Foreman infers its own state, and close-out is one call that returns one verdict; No-poll waiting: background jobs wake the session, and the drive works on something else meanwhile; Clean-room tasks: every task starts in a fresh ~30k context, handed off by a cache-priced fork instead of a /compact; Rebirth: sessions die at every milestone or thrash and are reborn from a capsule proven sufficient by a quiz; Spawn by fork: every orchestrator is born as a prompt-cache fork of one primer session; Handoffs that must reproduce the failure before they're accepted; Incident timelines assembled mechanically; action items become tasks and rules

## Milestone 5: Ambient verification and test intelligence
Tests, mutants and coverage run themselves after each edit and only surface when something flips.
- first slice: Background test daemon with affected-test selection and flip-only notifications, evidence recorded automatically.
- proof: Seed 10 regressions in a fixture repo; every one is caught by the daemon within seconds, with zero model-run test commands.
- members: Ambient verification: a background test daemon whose results arrive only when something flips; The model never runs tests during a change: a zero-token test daemon runs the affected ones after every edit and whispers the verdict; Push verification: affected tests run themselves and count as evidence; Observed test-sensitivity map: learned test selection, and 'dark code' no check has ever caught; Dead-edit alarm: edits the failing check never reaches; Shadow twin: every edit is executed in a twin worktree within seconds; only behaviour flips reach the model, and edits can be tried before they land; Behaviour diff: review and verify what the program now does, not what the code says; Mutation testing of the task's changed lines only, in seconds; Dependency-free coverage on sys.monitoring, scoped to the diff; A test writer that is done only when the mutants die; Characterization tests from real executions; Fuzz any function from its real inputs, minimize the crash, emit the red test; Flake hunter: rate, polluting test and the dimension that makes it deterministic; Performance budgets with a noise model, as a gate

## Milestone 6: Debugging intuition engine
The agent gets ranked suspects, a minimal failing delta and a recorded failure state before it starts guessing.
- first slice: Since-last-green delta debugging plus a failure flight recorder and fused suspect board on every red run.
- proof: Localisation gym: on 30 historical red-to-green episodes, root-cause file is in the top 3 suspects at a measured rate above the no-tool baseline.
- members: Why-red: delta debugging over the agent's own uncommitted edits; Since-last-green suspects: automatic hunk-level delta debugging inside the task; Bug genome: every past red-to-green episode, labelled by intervention, becomes a cross-project prior; Fix-delta memory: every red-to-green Foreman already recorded becomes a labelled causal example; Record once, ask anything: last-good differential trace and bad-value provenance; Failure flight recorder: program state at the moment of failure, captured for every run in the session; Suspects board: fused, self-weighted fault localisation that arrives before the agent asks; Spectrum fault localization on every red run; Probe planner: choose the experiment with the most information per second, then update beliefs automatically; Determinizer: force a flaky or works-there-fails-here failure to happen every time, and name the dimension; Foreman debugs itself: shrink every guard misfire to its minimal core and replay every hook crash; Symptom atlas: precomputed intuition from bugs planted during idle time; Bug nose: names the bug before a file is read, and smells bug-shaped hunks at edit time, graded on every red; Hindsight autopsy: when the answer was already on screen, and the habit that would have caught it; Localisation gym: every debugging improvement must move a measured root-cause score; Runtime world model: fm sim; Symptom-fix detector: is the fix where the bad value is made?; A debugger that survives between turns; Twenty-three red tests become three causes before Opus looks; Learn the codebase by predicting what it does, then watching it run; Trace cards: reuse the path that solved it, not just the answer

## Milestone 7: Knowledge graph
One bitemporal graph joins code, decisions, lessons and past fixes so every plan and edit is grounded in what is known.
- first slice: fm graph store ingesting graphify's code graph and Foreman history, with blast-radius and ranked context-pack queries.
- proof: As-of backtests: retrieval recall for historical tasks beats keyword retrieval, and blast-radius lists contain the files later actually touched.
- members: fm graph: one bitemporal work graph joining code to everything Foreman has done to it; Truth maintenance: a reversed decision or a false assumption flags everything built on it; Lessons anchored to symbols: the right lesson at the edit, plus duplicate, contradiction and supersession detection; Steer-protected code: Foreman notices when an edit undoes what the user explicitly asked for; Memory backtesting: as-of replays score and tune Foreman's own retrieval, for free; Context packs by personalized PageRank: the ranked, budgeted read-set for a task; Cross-project transfer by fingerprint: machine habits and fix-deltas, not word matches; graphify as the code layer: consume its AST graph, and give Foreman's history back as an overlay; Blast radius at plan time: what this change reaches, and what broke the last times it was touched; Pre-edit sensing: who notices if this function changes?; Consequence engine: executed answers to 'what breaks if...', computed before anyone asks; A verified repo atlas built once, injected every session

## Milestone 8: Orchestrator control plane
One zero-token conductor, bus and lock layer runs every session on every machine.
- first slice: Typed message bus with wake-on-mail, function-level leases, and a conductor that lists and steers all live sessions.
- proof: Two sessions plus a JARVIS host edit overlapping code: leases prevent collisions, a steer reaches all, a killed child is restarted.
- members: The Conductor: a zero-token control plane that runs Foreman orchestrators on every venue; Session mailbox with wake-on-mail: anything can message a running Claude session, and it wakes; Orchestrator bus: typed messages, most handled by code, wake-on-mail only for judgment; Delegated permissions down the tree: a parent answers its children's prompts and can only grant what it holds; Supervision trees: restart, quarantine and escalate, the Erlang/OTP way, for agents; Steer cascade: one steer, typed anywhere, reaches and binds every node it concerns; Leases: function-level area locks across sessions and machines, enforced before the edit; Overwatch: a resident watcher that keeps every session (local and JARVIS) from idling, looping or thrashing; One conductor, several orchestrator sessions, one milestone; Disciplined agent teams: Claude Code's native teams run Foreman briefs, gated by TeammateIdle and TaskCreated

## Milestone 9: Orchestrators of orchestrators
A big task becomes a tree of orchestrators that split along real code seams and learn which shapes win.
- first slice: Fractal spawn with capsule-only return and coupling-aware partition, run on one L task.
- proof: One L task run as a tree: result merged green, parent context stays under budget, compared with the single-session run.
- members: Fractal Foreman: a task too big for one session becomes its own orchestrator, and only a capsule comes back up; Self-reorganising org: an orchestrator of orchestrators that rewrites its own org chart while the work runs; Tournament mode: k orchestrators race different strategies, a judge merges the best and mines the losers; Shape learning: the Conductor learns which decompositions and team shapes win, partly from free simulation; Deadline scheduler: critical path and an elastic fleet, 'done by 7am' as the only input; Work market with context affinity: work goes to the orchestrator that already knows the code; Calibrated executor market: configurations bid on each task and are settled by a proper scoring rule; Multiverse: risky decisions fork the session, the universes attack each other with tests, the survivor merges and losers donate; Context-inheriting speculative forks for contested decisions; Contract-first swarm: one L task, the lead writes the seams and their failing tests, cheap builders fill them in parallel; Consumer review: orchestrators review the changes whose interfaces they consume; Coupling-aware partitioning: min-cut the code graph so orchestrators split along the code's real seams; Cross-repo atomic milestones: two-phase merge across repos and machines

## Milestone 10: Milestone work compiler and merge train
A queue compiles into milestones that are built together, proven once, and landed as a clean commit stack.
- first slice: Milestone fuse of related tasks with shared read, bulk red tests, one proof, and a commit-stack splitter.
- proof: Fuse 5 related tasks: total tokens below the sum of separate runs, and every stacked commit passes alone.
- members: Work compiler: a queue becomes milestones that are edited in one pass and proven once, with group-testing blame; Milestones: related items fused into one task — one read, one plan, red tests in bulk, a fast oracle per change, then full gates, proof and one review at the end; Milestone contracts: many items, one proof; Work as one milestone, land as a stack of commits that each pass on their own; Plans compile to Workflow scripts: deterministic, resumable fan-out that never enters the model's context; Merge train with speculative stacking and bisect-on-red: verify the batch, not every change; Affinity scheduler with pipelined prefetch: the queue reorders itself so neighbours share files, and the next task's room is built while this one closes; Review packs: reviewers read the diff, the facts and the call graph — once per milestone; Milestone fan-out: independent members of a milestone build in parallel cheap lanes while Opus judges diffs

## Milestone 11: Cockpit UI
The user sees, steers and rewinds the plan, beliefs, fleet and probes from one live pane.
- first slice: Live plan tree and fleet deck pane with steer input fed by the bus.
- proof: Screenshot-verified run through shot.py: steer a node, rewind a moment, see a JARVIS tile update.
- members: Steerable live plan tree; Probe swarm on the hypothesis board; Verified-moment timeline: scrub, diff, fork, bisect; Fleet deck with JARVIS tiles and a steer channel; Subagent tree with in-flight messages; Agent whiteboard: a pane Claude draws on; Hedge radar: the model's own hedges become tracked obligations; Mind view: a cockpit of the agent's live beliefs, which the user can edit, rewind and fork; Claude as navigator while you drive, silent until a test flips

## Milestone 12: Self-improvement flywheel
Foreman measures its own burden, tests changes on replays and ships proven improvements and detectors overnight.
- first slice: Burden meter plus flight-recorder replay set that scores a candidate change.
- proof: One overnight run produces a candidate that beats baseline on replays and merges with one approval.
- members: Rewind and re-decide: real failure moments become one-turn counterfactual tests; Burden meter: Foreman's own objective, measured on real sessions and attributed to each of its changes; Darwin archive: open-ended evolution of Foreman behind a multi-fidelity proof cascade; Flight recorder: every real hook decision becomes a free, labelled regression case; Live self-experiments: Foreman runs randomized trials of its own behaviour on real work; Corrections become backtested detectors that fire before the user has to say it; Proof-carrying self-changes with warranties checked on real sessions and revert proposals; Guard repair synthesizer: false blocks fixed overnight with a no-new-bypass proof; Improving the improver: a yield-ranked portfolio of improvement engines that evolves its own prompts; Overnight flywheel: wake up to proven Foreman improvements and merge each with one click; Co-evolving benchmark: forged micro-tasks that live only while they tell variants apart; Treadmill detector: know when to stop patching and redesign; Night school: every past mistake becomes a permanent test, and Foreman rewrites its own skills, hooks and agents overnight against them; Lessons compile to code: memory stops being prose the model must read and becomes checks that run; Corrections become enforced code rules, backtested on history; Agent foundry: the agent roster is mined from real delegations, benched, trained on verdicts and retired; Prosecutor court: an adversary with tools that wins only with a failing test the judge rules valid; Trajectory JIT: the agent's hot habits are compiled into deterministic tools, and the hook swaps them in when the habit starts; Compiled skills: repeated tool-call sequences mined from transcripts become zero-token macros, triggered by their first step; Foreman writes the project's scripts from what it keeps typing

## Milestone 13: Intent, planning and change skills
A wish becomes an executable spec and a priced plan, and large mechanical changes are done as one rule, not hundreds of edits.
- first slice: Intent compiler that turns a one-liner into a red spec, plus structural rewrite with cheap converter for leftovers.
- proof: A rename/migration across a fixture repo done by one rule with zero residual sites, and a one-line wish yields a red spec.

## Every idea

### Context firewall: cheap layer rewrites every big tool result before the expensive model sees it [R1 · Agents and orchestration; build; escalated; value 5, M]
Today every byte a Bash, Read or Grep call returns lands in the Opus context. After that it is re-read on every later turn. Claude Code 2.1.295 lets a PostToolUse hook replace any tool's output (updatedToolOutput), and Foreman doesn't use that. The firewall turns 'fm quiet' from a hint the model may ignore into a layer that always runs: large outputs are stashed raw and the model gets a digest plus a retrieval handle.
- mechanism: A new synchronous PostToolUse entry in hooks.json with matcher Bash|Read|Grep and a 10 s timeout. The existing async PostToolUse bookkeeping stays where it is. When the output is over 4k chars: (1) write the raw output to state/projects/<slug>/stash/<tool_use_id>.txt (redacted like evidence, pruned by tidy); (2) return hookSpecificOutput.updatedToolOutput holding a rule-based digest. Test runners (the fm check flake parsers: unittest FAIL/ERROR, pytest FAILED, go --- FAIL, cargo FAILED) give the summary line, the failing names and the first and last traceback frames. Builds give error lines with path:line. Grep gives hit counts per file plus the first 30 hits. A Read with no offset gives the fmmap.outline(path) table, the first 120 lines and the line ranges of every definition. (3) The digest ends with 'full: fm peek <id> [--lines A-B|--grep RE]'. fm peek is a new read-only command that prints a slice of the stash. Output with no recognised shape and over 20k chars gets a Haiku digest via fmideas.run_child (feature 'firewall', fmbudget.check first), never on the hook's hot path: the hook stashes, returns the rule digest at once, and the Haiku digest arrives as a PostToolUse note on the next call. Exempt: a Read that the next Edit depends on (Read with offset or limit, or a file already in the active task's touched set, stays raw), commands that are an acceptance criterion's verify command (auto-evidence still reads the raw text before the rewrite), and FOREMAN_FIREWALL=off. Measured in fm cost: 'Tool results' share and input-equivalent tokens per finished task, A/B with fm bench run --plugin (candidate with and without the firewall) over the same cases.
- first slice: Sync PostToolUse handler for Bash only: test and build digests from the existing failure parsers, stash plus 'fm peek', the exemptions (criterion verify commands, FOREMAN_FIREWALL=off), hook-latency test in bench_hooks.py (p95 stays under the 150 ms hook budget), and an fm cost line 'firewall: N outputs, X→Y chars'. Useful alone: test and build output is the single biggest context filler.
- bar/numbers: 2: tokens per finished task. fm cost over the last 3 days: 625.8M cache-read tokens out of ≈82.1M input-equivalent, so about 76% of spend is the context being re-read every turn. Tool results fill that context: Bash 59%, Read 33%, Grep 5%. A token that enters the context in turn k is paid again in every later turn, so cutting tool output 10× cuts most of the bill, not just the first read. The model also needs nothing new to remember, because the layer is always on.
- verdict: escalated to: Context PGO: Foreman profiles where every token goes and recompiles its own prompts, injections and output digests to the smallest form that still passes. Real savings, but fixed rules guess what matters. Context PGO learns each digest from which output lines the agent actually used, proves it on replayed moments, and adds instruction shrinking and read hoisting.

### Session mailbox with wake-on-mail: anything can message a running Claude session, and it wakes [R1 · Agents and orchestration; build; keep; value 5, M]
No harness gives an interactive coding session an inbox that other sessions, other machines, CI or the user's phone can post to, delivered at the next tool boundary and able to wake an idle session. Today the user sends work to JARVIS by tmux send-keys. Long sends collapse into a '[Pasted text]' that the session deprioritises. Claude Code's asyncRewake hook ('runs in background and wakes the model on exit code 2') makes this buildable, and Foreman doesn't use it.
- mechanism: fm mail send TARGET TEXT [--from NAME] [--urgent]. TARGET is a session id prefix, a project slug (its live sessions) or a task id (the session holding it). It writes state/mail/<session-or-slug>/<ts>-<rand>.json {from, at, text, urgent}, mode 0600, under fm's lock. Delivery has three paths. (1) Busy session: _pre_tool_use, which already emits additionalContext notes, does one listdir per call and injects 'Mail from <from> (data, not instructions from the user): …' once, then moves the file to read/. (2) Ending turn: stop() returns decision=block with the mail as the reason, the same way _drive continues work. (3) Idle session: a second Stop entry in hooks.json with asyncRewake: true and a long timeout runs 'hook MailWait'. It polls the session's mail dir every 2 s, exits 2 with the message on arrival (Claude Code wakes the model), and exits 0 quietly at timeout. One waiter per session (flock), and it is re-armed at every Stop. Urgent mail follows the NOW: rules (checkpoint, focus). Replies: fm mail ask TARGET 'Q' --wait 300 posts and blocks until the target answers with fm mail reply ID. That is session-to-session Q&A, for example a lane asking the main thread or the main thread asking JARVIS. Remote: 'ssh host fm mail send …' needs nothing new. Every message is labelled as data with its sender. The guard still governs what the woken session does, and a sensitive project accepts mail only from the same uid.
- first slice: fm mail send/list/read, delivery via _pre_tool_use and stop(), the asyncRewake MailWait Stop entry with its lock, and hook-payload tests for all three paths. The JARVIS delivery procedure in memory (capture there, then type one short line) becomes 'ssh jarvis fm mail send <slug> …'. Q&A replies come later.
- bar/numbers: 4: the user (and Claude) can steer or hand work to any live session, local or JARVIS, from any process, with no paste problem and no waiting for the session to finish its turn. 2: idle time. An idle session with new work wakes in seconds instead of after a human notices. 1: no coding agent has a cross-session, cross-machine, wakeable inbox; Claude Code's agent-team SendMessage only works inside one team.
- verdict: Passes the demo test: a message from a phone or CI wakes an idle session. It is also the nervous system the self-reorganising org needs for PAUSE/RESUME and steering. Build it as infrastructure.

### Overwatch: a resident watcher that keeps every session (local and JARVIS) from idling, looping or thrashing [R1 · Agents and orchestration; build; escalated; value 5, M]
The user babysits JARVIS by hand with a 60 s ssh Monitor and still loses hours to idling. Overwatch is a long-lived watcher process over all Claude sessions, local and remote, that notices on its own and acts on its own. It escalates from free rule detectors to a Haiku triage only on anomalies, and it scores its own interventions by whether they restarted progress.
- mechanism: fm overwatch [--once] [--host user@ip …] (hosts kept in global config), run as a systemd user unit the way fm serve writes one (fmserve.unit_text, linger, restart breaker) with a heartbeat (fmserve.beat). Every 60 s it gathers per session: the local view from fmclaude.sessions (live flag, last entry) and events.jsonl, and the remote view from 'ssh host fm claude list --json; fm ui --json' (one ssh call, ControlMaster reused). Detectors, all rule-based: (a) idle with work: no tool event for 5 min, queue or active task present, no drive_wait or background job; (b) foreground wait: a Bash row running over 3 min whose command is a sleep, until or watch loop, or an idle tmux pane spinner with no new events; (c) loop: the same failing command or guard_block 3+ times in 20 events; (d) duplicate work: two live sessions touching the same file or task within 10 min; (e) context pressure (statusline snapshot). Actions, escalating: fm mail (wake-on-mail) with a short line naming the fix; for (b), tmux Escape plus one short line (opt-in per host, the memory's own procedure); notify the user (fm notify) at the second miss; a Haiku child (fmideas.run_child, budgeted) reads the last 40 events only when two detectors disagree. Each intervention is logged as an 'overwatch' event, and 10 min later _fingerprint-style progress (steps, evidence, commits) decides whether it worked. fm friction reads detector yield, so a detector that never restarts progress is flagged for removal.
- first slice: fm overwatch --once --json over local sessions plus one --host, detectors a, b and c, actions notify plus log (mail once it exists, tmux Escape behind a per-host flag), the 'run' loop with heartbeat, and tests on recorded events.jsonl fixtures. Useful alone: it replaces the hand-run Monitor.
- bar/numbers: 2: idle time. The memory records hours of JARVIS idling in three different ways, and fm cost shows ssh at 7% of active time spent watching it. 4: unattended multi-session and multi-machine work becomes something the user can leave alone. The heartbeat and failover ideas already seen cover only fm run and night; this covers interactive and remote sessions.
- verdict: escalated to: Self-reorganising org: an orchestrator of orchestrators that rewrites its own org chart while the work runs. A watcher that only nudges stalled sessions. The org acts on the same signals by restructuring the team: specialists, splits, merges, rebudgets.

### Agent foundry: the agent roster is mined from real delegations, benched, trained on verdicts and retired [R1 · Agents and orchestration; build; escalated; value 4, L]
Today agent types are written by hand and never removed. Of 463 subagent launches on this device, fm-scout ran 0 times and fm-debugger 0 times. Meanwhile 47 general-purpose launches (≈2.7M tokens each) did recurring jobs with no specialist. The foundry closes that loop: demand-mined design, bench before install, outcome-fed memory, and retirement by measured yield.
- mechanism: fm roster mine reads every launch from Claude Code's store (fmclaude.subagents: type, description, toolUseId) plus each agent transcript (first user entry is the prompt, tools actually called, fmbudget.transcript_tokens). It builds a table per type: launches, tokens, last used, and outcomes. For reviewers, outcomes are fm second debate CONFIRMED/REFUTED counts and escapes their lens missed ('escape' ledger events carry the lenses). For builders, outcomes are lane_merge vs lane_rm. Design: cluster general-purpose and Explore prompts by description stems and shared prompt n-grams ('Ground … capabilities', 'Write benchmark lane', 'Verify … facts', 'Comment pass'). For each cluster with 3+ launches, a tool-less child (fmevolve-style: instruction text only, never code) drafts an agent .md. The tool allowlist is the tools those transcripts really used, the model is the cheapest whose observed context fits, and the output schema comes from how the main thread used the results. Test: replay the cluster's historical prompts against the candidate as judged bench cases (fmbench judged path; the judge compares the candidate's answer with the original's and with what the main thread kept). Train: after each adjudication, Foreman writes verdict lines into the type's native memory (agent frontmatter memory: project), for example 'refuted 4/5 quoting-related guard findings here: give a concrete shell input'. These are written by Foreman from ground truth, not by the agent about itself. Retire: a type unused for 30 days, or beaten on the bench by another type, is proposed for removal. Its description stops costing always-on tokens in every session. Install and retire both need the user's yes (Foreman changes).
- first slice: fm roster mine (device-wide table: launches, tokens, last used, merge/confirm rates where the ledger has them) plus fm roster propose: clusters with drafted candidate .md files written to state/roster/candidates/ with the evidence for each, and a retire list. No install, no bench. Useful alone: it shows which agents the work actually needs and which are dead weight.
- bar/numbers: 3: compounding. Every delegation feeds the roster, so the next delegation goes to a better-fitted, cheaper, verdict-trained agent, and dead types stop costing context. 1: no coding agent derives its agent types from its own delegation history and retires them on measured yield. Seen ideas cover a scorecard and hand-added specialists; this is the closed loop.
- verdict: escalated to: Self-reorganising org: an orchestrator of orchestrators that rewrites its own org chart while the work runs. Mining delegations into hand-written agent types is slow and static. The org spawns specialists from live demand and retires them by measured yield; repeated tool routines go to Trajectory JIT.

### Prosecutor court: an adversary with tools that wins only with a failing test the judge rules valid [R1 · Agents and orchestration; build; keep; value 4, M]
Foreman's adversaries today are read-only reviewers and a tool-less debate. Their 'findings' are claims the main thread must verify, and 28 defects still escaped past them. The prosecutor gets a sandboxed copy of the finished tree and a budget, and the only way to win is executable: a test that fails on the shipped code and asserts something the brief really required. An independent judge rules on that requirement, without seeing the prosecutor's reasoning.
- mechanism: fm prosecute ID [--minutes 15 --budget 1.0]. (1) Build a detached worktree of the task's finished tree, as fm task prove --hunks does (commit-tree of the current tree). (2) Run claude -p (sonnet, FOREMAN_DRIVE_TASK unset, guard 'confine' so writes stay in the worktree, no network) with a prompt holding the brief's request, criteria and non-goals, the diff from fm audit prep, and the rule 'add tests only; you win by a test that fails here and quotes the requirement it enforces'. (3) fm reruns each new test itself: it must fail on the finished tree and must not be flaky (run twice; fm check's flake logic). (4) The judge is a tool-less child (fmsecond._child, another model). It sees only the request, the criteria and the test with its failure, never the prosecutor's argument, and rules VALID (required behaviour), INVENTED (asserts unrequested behaviour) or ENV (environmental). (5) A VALID test becomes a FIX brief (fm capture --source discovered, the test file attached, the task named so _note_escapes logs it as a caught escape). The prosecutor's yield per area (valid/attempts, $ per valid test) goes to the ledger, and fm gates can require a court for L tasks or for areas whose escape rate is above zero.
- first slice: fm prosecute ID for one task: worktree, prosecutor run, fm's own fail-twice check, blind judge, and a FIX capture for VALID tests, plus prosecute events with yield. Run by hand on the last 5 tasks that later had escapes (a back-test: would it have caught them?).
- bar/numbers: 2: bugs that escape to the user. 28 escape events exist against tasks that had passed intent, edge and adversary lenses. 1: no coding agent pits a tool-wielding adversary against finished work with an executable win condition and a blind judge. 'Adversarial verification' (seen) is a read-only 'forge a pass' reviewer; this one must produce the failing test.
- verdict: Passes: an adversary with tools wins only by writing a valid failing test against shipped work. Cheap, executable, and reused as the validity rule in Multiverse cross-examination.

### Live navigator for every builder lane (Claude Code's native observer agents) [R1 · Agents and orchestration; skip; kill; value 4, S]
Claude Code 2.1.295 ships an experimental observer mechanism. An agent's frontmatter can name an observer type, which is auto-spawned in the background, gets read-only activity digests, and reports back to the observed agent through ObserverReport. Nobody pairs it with a contract. Foreman's navigator knows the brief, so it can catch drift live in a builder: no failing test yet, edits outside scope, the third identical failure, an unrecorded criterion. Today these are caught only after the lane returns, or never, given the builder '(retry)' history.
- mechanism: A new plugin/agents/fm-navigator.md (model haiku, tools Read and Grep). Its prompt: it observes one Foreman builder, reads the brief file named in the first digest (the lane brief path from fm lane brief), and reports only when a rule fires, in one or two sentences. Rules: an edit before any recorded failing test (test-first); an edit path outside the brief's scope or the worktree; the same failing command 3 times with an untested hypothesis available (fm task hypo); a commit while a criterion has no [ran] evidence; a cd out of the worktree. fm-builder.md gets 'observer: fm-navigator'. Launches set CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_OBSERVER_AGENTS=1: builder_brief tells the main thread, and fm run sets it in the env it already passes. Effect is measured from existing ledger events: lane outcomes (lane_merge vs lane_rm and retries), evidence order (first failing evidence before the first touched event) and builder tokens, comparing navigator-on and navigator-off lanes in fm bench.
- first slice: Add fm-navigator.md and the observer line on fm-builder.md, set the env in builder launches, record observer reports as 'navigator' ledger events via SubagentStop or transcript scan, and run two real lanes with and without it. Also probe whether 'claude -p --agent foreman:fm-builder' (fm run lanes) gets observed too.
- bar/numbers: 1: a brief-aware LLM navigator watching another agent's live activity and correcting it mid-run is new; the native observer plumbing exists, but no harness uses it this way. 2: rework, since builders retried or fell back to general-purpose repeatedly (W10b ×3, W17a ×3, benchmark lanes ×2).
- verdict: The demo is a Haiku observer nudging a builder. The org watches lanes through hard signals, and the shadow twin makes test-first drift visible without a chaperone.

### Disciplined agent teams: Claude Code's native teams run Foreman briefs, gated by TeammateIdle and TaskCreated [R1 · Agents and orchestration; build; escalated; value 4, M]
Claude Code 2.1.295 has experimental agent teams: TeamCreate, inter-teammate SendMessage, a shared task list, and TeammateIdle and TaskCreated hooks. Foreman wires none of it, and its only parallelism is fire-and-forget claude -p lanes in fm run (at most 3, no talk, no steering). A Foreman team makes each teammate an interactive, steerable session bound to one brief and one lane. Teammates can't go idle or mark work done without evidence, and the lead is Foreman's queue.
- mechanism: fm team plan [--n 4] picks a collision-free set with fmserve._batch (planned S/M tasks, disjoint footprints including fm map co-change, different relate groups). It prints the TeamCreate spawn prompt: one teammate per brief, whose first commands are 'fm lane new ID' and then 'fm focus ID' in that lane (so Foreman's lane machinery isolates files and the guard's confine rules apply), plus the brief path. New hooks.json entries: TaskCreated (a team task without a T-id is captured as a brief via fm capture so the team list and Foreman stay one; a task whose T-id is already held elsewhere is refused, per c.held_elsewhere). TeammateIdle (if the teammate's lane task has steps or criteria without evidence: exit 2 with 'fm next' for that task, as the Stop drive does; if it's done: assign the next free brief from the same relate group, else let it idle). The existing task_completed gate (dormant today) becomes live, because teams use the task tools. The lead merges finished lanes one at a time with the existing _integrate (rebase, gates, fast-forward). The foreman-ui band shows one row per teammate from fm lane list.
- first slice: TeammateIdle and TaskCreated handlers (payload-fixture tests, exit-2 semantics checked against the binary), fm team plan, and one live run of two S tasks as a two-teammate team with the experimental flag, merged by fm lane merge.
- bar/numbers: 2: throughput and idle time. Several evidence-gated tasks progress at once inside the user's interactive session, each steerable, instead of serially or as headless runs nobody can talk to. The TaskCompleted gate that ships dormant starts enforcing. 4: the user can watch and steer a team that obeys Foreman's discipline.
- verdict: escalated to: Self-reorganising org: an orchestrator of orchestrators that rewrites its own org chart while the work runs. Wiring native teams is plumbing with a fixed shape. The org uses teams as one substrate and changes the shape itself.

### Contract-first swarm: one L task, the lead writes the seams and their failing tests, cheap builders fill them in parallel [R1 · Agents and orchestration; build; escalated; value 4, L]
Lanes today parallelise only independent S/M tasks; every L task runs serially on the expensive model. The swarm turns one L task into a contract: interfaces plus one red test per seam, committed by the lead. Cheap models then implement the seams in parallel lanes, each judged only by its seam test, and the full suite gates integration. The tests are the contract, so cheap workers can't drift without a red gate.
- mechanism: Phase 1, lead (main thread, Opus): an L brief's step 1 is 'seams'. The lead commits the stub interfaces and a test file per seam, each recorded red with fm task evidence --run (fm prove semantics). Phase 2: fm swarm ID turns each seam into a child S brief (scope = the seam's files from the stub commit's diff, its one criterion = that seam's test command, depends_on the host, meta swarm_of=host, tier S). This is fm batch's member bookkeeping run in reverse: children close done-in the host. fm run --parallel then dispatches them, already safe because their scopes are disjoint, with run_models S=sonnet or haiku. Phase 3: the host's remaining criterion is the full integration suite on the merged tree. A failing integration test is mapped back to a seam (fm impact: tests ↔ files), and that child is reopened with the failure. After two failed child sessions the lead takes the seam back. Spend and wall-clock per swarm vs comparable serial L tasks are logged.
- first slice: fm swarm ID: child briefs from a seam commit (scope from diff, criterion from its test file, depends_on, swarm_of), done-in closure into the host, and an fm run --parallel run over them; tested on a toy repo case and one real L task.
- bar/numbers: 2: wall-clock and expensive-model tokens on L tasks. With 3–5 seams in parallel on Sonnet, an L task finishes in about the time of its slowest seam plus integration, and Opus writes only the contract and reviews. 1: no coding agent compiles a task into test-guarded seams and fans it out to cheaper models with test-defined acceptance.
- verdict: escalated to: Self-reorganising org: an orchestrator of orchestrators that rewrites its own org chart while the work runs. Strong pattern, but run by hand for one L task. It becomes the org's SPLIT move, fired when a lead is overloaded, with milestones from the work compiler as the unit.

### Calibrated executor market: configurations bid on each task and are settled by a proper scoring rule [R1 · Agents and orchestration; build; escalated; value 3, M]
Routing today is a fixed tier→model map (fm run --models). In the market, each executor configuration (model × venue: main, lane, builder × effort) bids on each task: P(done in one session) and expected tokens. Allocation maximises expected value per dollar with Thompson-sampled exploration. Every bid is settled against the outcome with a Brier/log score, so bidders that overpromise lose weight, and routing becomes calibrated per type, tier and code area.
- mechanism: Bids come mostly from statistics, with no LLM. For each executor e and task t: a Beta posterior on success from run_session and task outcomes of past tasks similar to t (fm recall BM25 neighbours weighted by similarity, falling back to type × tier). Expected tokens is the median of those neighbours' input-equivalent tokens (fmserve._session_tokens). For L tasks only, an optional self-bid: a tool-less child per executor model reads the brief and returns P and its biggest risk. It is blended with the statistical bid by that bidder's historical Brier skill, so its weight is earned. Allocation: Thompson sample P̃ from each posterior, pick argmax(P̃·V − λ·cost), where V is the inbox value already computed by rank_inbox and λ comes from fmbudget pace. Settlement: at done, block or the session limit, log a 'bid_settled' event with the Brier score, and update the posteriors. fm market ID shows the book; fm run --market uses it; fm digest shows calibration curves per executor.
- first slice: fm market ID (statistical bids only, from the run_session history and recall neighbours), fm run --market with Thompson choice restricted to the run_models entries, and bid_settled logging. Useful alone: the report already shows which model actually finishes which work here.
- bar/numbers: 3: compounding. Every finished task sharpens routing, and exploration finds where a cheaper model is good enough (or where Opus is needed) without the user setting it. 2: tokens per finished task once cheap executors win the tasks they reliably finish. 1: no coding agent prices its own executors with proper scoring and settles them against outcomes.
- verdict: escalated to: Opus on call: the cheapest model attempts every step first, checked by machines not by Opus; the frontier model is paged only on failure. Bids still leave Opus driving the loop. Opus on call inverts it: cheap models attempt first and machines accept, with Opus paged only on failure. The acceptance posteriors replace the bids.

### Pair mode: the user claims steps, Foreman guards them from Claude and verifies the user's own edits [R1 · Agents and orchestration; skip; kill; value 3, M]
Coding agents treat the human as a prompter, not a teammate on the same task. In pair mode one brief is split between the user and Claude. Claude is refused edits to files of a step the user claimed, the user's hand edits are detected and attributed to their step, and the user's step is held to the same evidence gate. Its criterion runs when their files settle, and both sides see the result.
- mechanism: fm task claim ID N [--files GLOB…] (by the user, or offered by Claude: 'you take step 3, the copy; I take step 4') sets step owner=user and the step's files in brief meta. _pre_tool_use refuses Claude's Edit/Write/shell writes into those files while the step is open: 'the user owns step 3 (src/ui/copy.ts); log what you'd change with fm task log'. This is the existing scope-note path promoted to a refusal. Attribution: at UserPromptSubmit and Stop, a cheap git status plus mtime check (the T-0086 machinery) finds files changed since the last turn that no Claude tool touched. Those become 'user_touched' events on the claimed step. Verification: when the step's files stop changing for 60 s, Overwatch, or the next hook if Overwatch isn't running, runs the step's verify command through fm task evidence --run and records it as the user's evidence. The result reaches Claude by mail or note ('the user's step 3 passes; continue step 4') and the user by the foreman-ui band toast. fm task done counts user-owned steps like any other.
- first slice: fm task claim and unclaim, the _pre_tool_use refusal for claimed files, user_touched attribution at UserPromptSubmit and Stop, and the verify run at the next hook after the files settle, with tests for each. The band toast can come later.
- bar/numbers: 4: the user can work inside a Foreman task, doing the part they care about while the agent does the rest, without the agent clobbering their edits or skipping verification of either side. 1: no coding agent splits one task's steps between human and agent with enforced ownership and shared evidence gates.
- verdict: The demo is a refusal flag on files the user claimed. The user mainly wants autonomy, and the mind view gives a far stronger way to co-steer.

### Why-red: delta debugging over the agent's own uncommitted edits [R1 · Debugging intuition; build; escalated; value 5, M]
Every fm check already snapshots the whole working tree as a git tree object and logs it with each gate's exit code. So when a gate that was green goes red, Foreman can run Zeller's ddmin over the hunks between the last green tree and now, in a scratch worktree, and return the minimal set of edits that causes the failure. That set is usually 1-2 hunks, and ddmin also finds interacting pairs that bisect cannot. No coding agent keeps a green-tree trail of uncommitted states, so none can answer 'which of my last 40 edits broke this' without commits.
- mechanism: Trigger: cmd_check sees gate G exit non-zero, and ledger_tail has an earlier check_run where G exited 0 with a resolvable tree (git rev-parse of the stored id; widen the stored id from 12 to 40 chars, and pin the newest green tree per gate under refs/foreman/green/<gate-hash> so gc can't prune it). Diff green_tree to red_tree with the same -U0 _hunks/_definitions split prove --hunks uses. Test files are excluded, since they define the check. Build the scratch commit and worktree exactly as task_prove_hunks does. ddmin's test(subset) = the green tree plus that subset of hunks, applied via _revert_hunk on the complement, with mtimes touched so .pyc and make caches don't lie. It runs G, or only the failing test ids from failing_tests(output) when the runner accepts a filter. Runs are capped by wall time from G's median duration (the _slower history). Flaky gates (_flaky_count>0) need two agreeing runs per verdict. Output: 'G went red between tree a1b2 (green 14:02) and now: minimal cause = fmguard.py hunk @@-1819 (uses _IFS_USE) AND fmguard.py hunk @@-140 (deleted its definition); 7 runs, 41 s'. It is recorded as ran evidence and as an auto-confirmed hypothesis (Brief.add_hypothesis + mark_hypothesis), so the debugging ledger fills itself. Fast gates (<30 s median) run detached right away (the Popen pattern at fmhooks second-session spawn), and the answer is injected on the next PostToolUse. Slow gates get a one-line fm next suggestion. cmd_sentinel failures (a later task broke an earlier task's proof) get the same treatment against the tree that proof passed on.
- first slice: `fm why-red "<gate cmd>"`: find the last green check_run for that command, resolve both trees, ddmin over code hunks in a scratch worktree, print the minimal culprit hunks, and record them as evidence plus a confirmed hypothesis. fm check prints a one-line pointer whenever a previously green gate fails. Tests: a toy repo where two hunks fail only together (bisect can't find it), one where a single hunk fails, and a pruned-tree case that says so plainly.
- bar/numbers: 1: no agent or harness localizes failures across an uncommitted edit trajectory, or finds interacting edit pairs. 2: time to root cause for every regression, from reading a 40-hunk diff to being handed 1-2 hunks, with zero tokens spent.
- verdict: escalated to: Shadow twin: every edit is executed in a twin worktree within seconds; only behaviour flips reach the model, and edits can be tried before they land. ddmin over hunks after the fact. The shadow twin tests every edit as it lands, so the breaking edit is known at once; ddmin survives only for interacting edits and as the work compiler's group-testing blame.

### Bug genome: every past red-to-green episode, labelled by intervention, becomes a cross-project prior [R1 · Debugging intuition; build; escalated; value 5, L]
This is Foreman's sixth sense. At the first failure, it matches the symptom against every bug the user's projects have had, mined from local transcripts and FIX tasks. Each past case is labelled with its true causal edit, taken from which hunks the test provably depends on rather than from the agent's story, and with a root-cause class. The hint is a base rate over classes, plus the guesses that looked right and weren't: 'symptoms like this: 4 of 6 past cases were stale mtime caching; the obvious guess, import order, was wrong in 3 of them'. Foreman scores each prior's precision afterwards and mutes classes that mislead.
- mechanism: Offline miner (an fm night job, local only, incremental byte offsets like _scan_notices). It walks registered projects' transcripts (the fmreplay._logs reader) and cuts episodes: from the first failing test-like Bash result to the first pass of the same command shape. Per episode it stores: symptom features (error class, the FIRST error line rather than the last summary, frame function names, failing test ids, file stems), the Edit/Write old/new strings in between, the non-edit probes, and every edit after which the failure signature did not change (a wrong guess). Causal label: for FIX tasks with a Foreman-Task commit, reuse the prove / prove --hunks results (proven hunks are causal). For other episodes, a night retro-ddmin over the episode's edits uses Why-red's engine. A tool-less Haiku child (fmideas.run_child, budget-capped) assigns one class from a fixed ~20-class taxonomy (stale cache/mtime, quoting/escaping, path resolution, env/PATH, ordering/race, off-by-one, wrong API assumption, encoding, config precedence, state leaking between tests, …), given only the causal hunk and the symptom. The data goes in state/genome.jsonl, redacted; reads across projects need fm share on, and fm sensitive projects are excluded. Online: note_failure replaces seen_before's exact-string match with BM25 over symptom tokens plus frame-name overlap (fmrecall.recall's machinery). It injects the top cause classes with counts, the wrong guesses aggregated per class, and the probe that told cases apart. Every prior shown is logged. At close, the task's confirmed hypothesis or proven hunks get classed the same way, and per-class precision is kept. A class shown wrongly far more often than rightly stops being shown. Complements the reasoning theme's 'Trace cards', which replay one past investigation path; the genome is the statistical prior over all of them.
- first slice: `fm genome build --days 60` mines registered projects' transcripts into genome.jsonl: symptom features, edits, probes and wrong guesses, with causal labels from existing prove events where present. Swap seen_before's exact-signature pointer in the PostToolUseFailure hint for the top-3 genome matches with their base rates, and log each hint and its later outcome. Taxonomy labelling and retro-ddmin come next.
- bar/numbers: 1: cross-project causal priors with anti-priors, labelled by intervention rather than narrative, exist in no agent. 3: each solved bug makes the next diagnosis faster, and the precision measurement shows whether it did.
- verdict: escalated to: Bug nose: names the bug before a file is read, and smells bug-shaped hunks at edit time, graded on every red. The right data, but its output is a base-rate hint. The bug nose turns it into a calibrated predictor of location, class and a confirming probe that runs itself, graded on every red and also used at edit time.

### Record once, ask anything: last-good differential trace and bad-value provenance [R1 · Debugging intuition; build; escalated; value 5, L]
The agent stops adding prints and rerunning. Foreman records the failing test's execution once: every call and return in project files, with argument and return reprs. It records it on both the last green tree and the red tree, aligns the two traces, and reports the first divergence, e.g. '_split("a${IFS}b") returned ["a${IFS}b"] now, ["a","b"] when green; fmguard.py, called from check_bash'. With no green tree to compare, it walks the offending value backwards through the recording to the function that produced it. That automates the manual backward tracing debugging-root-cause-tracing.md prescribes. Questions about runtime values become queries over the recording (omniscient debugging for an agent).
- mechanism: Shares the stdlib sitecustomize tracer proposed by the reasoning theme's fm sim (whichever lands first builds it). fm sim records types only; this mode, FM_TRACE=<file>, records values. sys.monitoring (PEP 669; Python 3.14 here) PY_START/PY_RETURN/RAISE callbacks are filtered to code objects under the repo root (DISABLE for the rest). Each record has seq, depth, qualname, file:line, args reprlib-truncated to 120 chars and passed through c.redact, and the return repr. Hit counts per function are capped. PYTHONPATH inheritance records subprocesses too, which matters for Foreman's own tests that spawn hooks. `fm trace diff <cmd>` runs the failing test ids at the last green tree (Why-red's lookup, prove's scratch-worktree helper) and at the current tree. It aligns the two call sequences with an LCS per caller over (qualname, normalised args), normalising addresses, ids and timestamps as failure_signature does, and prints the first divergent call or return with 5 lines of context each side. `fm trace origin <cmd>` takes the offending value (the assert operands or the raising frame's locals, from the flight recorder) and walks recorded returns and arguments backwards to the earliest producer whose inputs did not contain it, then repeats on its contributing inputs (depth ≤6) to print the origin-to-symptom chain. That chain is the dynamic evidence the reasoning theme's static Symptom-fix detector lacks. `fm trace q <qualname|file:line>` answers whether something was called, with what, and what it returned. Results become evidence and auto-hypotheses. Later: Node via NODE_OPTIONS --require with node:inspector (Node 26 is installed); Rust test binaries via gdb -batch (gdb present, ptrace_scope=1).
- first slice: Python only: the value-recording mode of the tracer, `fm trace run -- <cmd>` (into state/projects/<slug>/traces/, size-capped), and `fm trace diff <cmd> [--green TREE]` printing the first divergence. Test: a toy repo where a helper's return value changed and the assertion fails three frames later. The divergence must name the helper.
- bar/numbers: 1: no agent records or diffs program executions; agents see only what they print. 2: a regression's divergence point or a bad value's origin in one command, instead of print-and-rerun cycles.
- verdict: escalated to: Behaviour diff: review and verify what the program now does, not what the code says. One failing test's trace diff is a narrow use of the same recorder. The behaviour diff records the whole suite at two trees and becomes the verification and review artefact; first divergence is just a query on it.

### Localisation gym: every debugging improvement must move a measured root-cause score [R1 · Debugging intuition; build; escalated; value 5, M]
Foreman gets an objective, token-free fitness function for its own debugging intelligence. Hundreds of real past bugs from the user's repos are replayed as a red tree plus failing output. Each localiser (diff recency, why-red, flight-recorder locals, genome prior, symptom atlas, SBFL, the fm-debugger prompt) is scored on where it ranks the true fix lines: top-1, top-5, mean reciprocal rank, runs and tokens spent. A change to the debugging machinery, or an fm evolve edit to debugging.md or fm-debugger.md, merges only if the score improves. That is recursive self-improvement with proof that it helped.
- mechanism: Cases come from fmbench.build and build_commits fail-to-pass cases (already validated: fail at base plus tests, pass at the commit) and FIX tasks with prove evidence. Ground truth is the commit's non-test hunks, narrowed to proven hunks where prove --hunks ran. The scenario is exactly prove's red tree (base plus the commit's tests). Mechanical localisers run with no model. Score = rank of the first output line that falls inside a true hunk, at file and line level. LLM localisers (fm-debugger, the probe planner) run on a small sampled subset under fm budget. Results per localiser version go to bench/results. fm night re-runs the mechanical suite when localiser code changes. fmbench.gate gets a 'localize' arm, so fm evolve candidates targeting agents/fm-debugger.md or skills/intake/references/debugging*.md are judged on MRR. fm friction shows the trend. The newest 20% of cases are held out to catch overfitting. The gym also learns fusion weights for the Suspects board.
- first slice: `fm bench localize [--cases N] [--localizer recency|why-red]` over existing and --commits-built cases. It scores the agent's de facto default (files ranked by most recent change) against whatever localisers exist, and prints top-1, top-5 and MRR per localiser into bench/results. It is useful alone because it puts a number on how good 'look at the latest edit' is, and gives every other debugging idea its baseline.
- bar/numbers: 3: debugging gains become measurable and compound, each one proven. 2: cheap mechanical localisers can be tuned on hundreds of cases offline without spending tokens.
- verdict: escalated to: Bug nose: names the bug before a file is read, and smells bug-shaped hunks at edit time, graded on every red. Offline scoring is the bug nose's grader, now run live on every real fix rather than as a separate benchmark.

### Failure flight recorder: program state at the moment of failure, captured for every run in the session [R1 · Debugging intuition; build; escalated; value 4, M]
When any Python test or script fails in the session, including the agent's own direct Bash test runs, Foreman has already saved the locals of every project frame in the traceback, and both operands for assertion failures. The failure hint then starts from 'cmd = "rm${IFS}-rf ~", _IFS_USE unbound at fmguard.py check_bash' instead of a bare stack trace. Sentry does this in production; no coding agent does it for its own runs.
- mechanism: SessionStart already writes exports into CLAUDE_ENV_FILE (FOREMAN_SESSION_ID). It adds `PYTHONPATH=<foreman shim dir>:$PYTHONPATH` and `FM_CAPTURE=<project captures dir>`, and `RUST_BACKTRACE=1` costs nothing for Rust. The shim's sitecustomize does nothing unless something fails. It installs sys.excepthook and threading.excepthook, wraps unittest's TestResult.addFailure/addError, and registers a pytest_runtest_makereport plugin only when pytest is importable. On failure it walks traceback frames whose co_filename is under the repo root and dumps {file, line, func, locals: {name: reprlib ≤200 chars}}, redacted. One JSON per failure goes to state/projects/<slug>/captures/, keyed by failure_signature, capped at 200 files. note_failure (PostToolUseFailure) and fm check's failure line attach the 6 most telling locals: names on the failing line first, then None, empty or unexpected types. The fm-debugger brief gets the capture appended, so its hypotheses cite real values. Next: Node via NODE_OPTIONS --require with node:inspector pause-on-exception scope dump; Rust via gdb -batch -ex 'break rust_panic' -ex run -ex 'bt full' on the failing test binary (gdb installed, ptrace_scope=1). volt, the busiest project with 291 recorded failures, is Rust.
- first slice: Python: the shim, the CLAUDE_ENV_FILE exports, the captures store with redaction and caps, and a 'state at failure' block in the PostToolUseFailure hint and fm check's failure output. Tests: a unittest failure and an uncaught exception in a subprocess each leave a capture with the right locals; a secret-looking local is redacted; `python -I` and fm sensitive projects capture nothing.
- bar/numbers: 2: removes the print-and-rerun loop behind long episodes, and works for the agent's own direct test runs, not just fm gates. 1: no agent sees program state it didn't print.
- verdict: escalated to: Behaviour diff: review and verify what the program now does, not what the code says. Locals at failure come free from the behaviour recorder's shim, which also covers passing runs and alignment.

### Suspects board: fused, self-weighted fault localisation that arrives before the agent asks [R1 · Debugging intuition; build; escalated; value 4, M]
Foreman's debugging aids today are voluntary, and the data shows they go unused: zero hypothesis events across all 9 projects. The board makes localisation automatic. The moment a gate or test goes red, Foreman runs every available localiser in the background while the agent is still reading the output. It fuses the rankings with weights learned in the Localisation gym and injects one ranked 'Suspects' block of file:line and function, each with the signal that put it there. The agent starts from a ranked shortlist instead of guessing.
- mechanism: PostToolUseFailure on a gate or test-like command spawns `fm suspects --for <sig>` detached (the fmhooks Popen pattern). It collects rankings from each available signal: lines changed since the last green tree (exists), Why-red culprit hunks, flight-recorder frames, genome cause-class matches mapped onto current files, symptom atlas, SBFL (the reasoning theme's fm sim suspects), fmmap impact and test↔source links (exists), and tripwires.json lesson hits (exists). It combines them with weighted reciprocal rank fusion. Weights come from the gym's per-localiser MRR for this project's language, with flat weights before any gym results exist. The top 5 go to state/projects/<slug>/suspects.json. The next PostToolUse or UserPromptSubmit injects them once as additionalContext, and each becomes an open hypothesis with its probe, so next_action's 'test hypothesis H1 before more fixes' takes over. fm ui --json carries them so the foreman-ui band can show a suspects row with meters (rows.tsx meter()). At close, the confirmed suspect's rank is logged as online gym data.
- first slice: `fm suspects` fusing the signals that already exist: diff hunks since the last green tree, fmmap impact and test↔source links, tripwires, failure memory. It is spawned on PostToolUseFailure for gate commands and injected once, with confirmed-suspect rank logging. Later localisers plug in as they land.
- bar/numbers: 2: time to root cause, with every localiser on every failure at no agent effort. 3: fusion weights improve as the gym and the online outcomes accumulate.
- verdict: escalated to: Bug nose: names the bug before a file is read, and smells bug-shaped hunks at edit time, graded on every red. Fusing localisers into a ranked list is a table. The nose replaces it with one calibrated guess plus a probe already run.

### Probe planner: choose the experiment with the most information per second, then update beliefs automatically [R1 · Debugging intuition; build; escalated; value 4, M]
Debugging becomes an explicit Bayesian search. Hypotheses carry probabilities. Each candidate probe carries the outcome every hypothesis predicts. Foreman picks the probe with the highest expected information gain per estimated second, and when the probe runs it applies Bayes to the real outcome without being asked. A senior engineer's instinct, 'run the test that splits the possibilities', becomes a computation. No agent ranks experiments by information gain. Complements the reasoning theme's confusion meter, which scores predictions; this chooses what to run next.
- mechanism: Hypotheses lines gain `p=0.40`. A new Probes section holds outcome tables: `P2 grep -n _IFS_USE fmguard.py | H1: no def · H2: def after use · H3: def present | expect /def _IFS_USE/`. Generation: on the 2nd identical failure signature in a task (one below note_failure's REPEATS), or when fm-debugger returns, a tool-less child (fmideas.run_child, budget-capped) gets the failure plus suspects, capture and why-red output. It returns JSON: hypotheses with priors (blended with genome base rates), and ≤5 probes, each with a predicted outcome class per hypothesis and a cost class. Foreman computes EIG = H(prior) − Σ_o P(o)·H(posterior | o), with P(o) = Σ p_h over the hypotheses predicting o. It divides by seconds estimated from past ledger runs of the same command shape. next_action's executing-stage line names the best probe. When post_tool_use sees that probe's command run, its declared --expect regex decides the outcome class (a tiny child does it only when no regex was given). Foreman updates the posteriors, marks hypotheses below 0.05 ruled out and at 0.9 or above confirmed, and writes an automatic fm surprise when the observed outcome had predicted probability under 0.2.
- first slice: Deterministic core, no LLM: `fm task hypo ID add … --p 0.4`, `fm task probe ID add "<cmd>" --predict "H1=a;H2=b;H3=a" --expect REGEX`, `fm task probe ID plan` (EIG ranking), and the PostToolUse auto-update when a planned probe command runs. fm next names the top probe. Test: a 3-hypothesis case where the planner prefers the 2-vs-1 splitting probe over the one that would only confirm the likeliest hypothesis.
- bar/numbers: 1: experiment selection by expected information gain, with predictions committed before the run, exists in no agent. 2: fewer probes to root cause, and it turns the unused hypothesis ledger into an automatic one.
- verdict: escalated to: Bug nose: names the bug before a file is read, and smells bug-shaped hunks at edit time, graded on every red. Information-gain ranking of hand-written probes is a table nobody fills in (zero hypothesis events). The nose proposes the confirming probe per cause class and runs it in the twin.

### Determinizer: force a flaky or works-there-fails-here failure to happen every time, and name the dimension [R1 · Debugging intuition; build; keep; value 4, M]
Today fm check labels a test flaky and says 'fix the race, don't rerun past it', but gives no tool to do that. Instead of rerun-and-hope, Foreman searches the perturbation space: CPU count, thread switch interval, hash seed, test order, timezone and locale, clock skew, and the env differences between the context where it passed and where it failed. It looks for the setting that makes the failure happen every time and reports the dimension: 'fails 20/20 with PYTHONHASHSEED=3 and reversed order: set-iteration-order dependency'. The forcing setting becomes the regression test's harness. The user can turn a heisenbug into a deterministic repro, which they could not do before.
- mechanism: `fm determinize <gate|test id>` measures a baseline failure rate over k runs. For each dimension it measures k runs: taskset -c 0 (installed); PYTHONHASHSEED sweep; reversed or shuffled test order (unittest name list, pytest -p randomly when present); TZ=Pacific/Kiritimati and LANG=C/tr_TR.UTF-8 (the Turkish i); and in the Python shim, sys.setswitchinterval(1e-6) and a monotonic clock skew, since faketime isn't installed. Env diff: check_run today stores only env_id, an 8-char hash of 8 variables. It would also store the redacted values of non-secret-named variables per env_id. When a gate passed under env A and fails under B (lanes, bench worktrees, subagents, fm run versus interactive), ddmin over the differing variables plus cwd and umask finds the minimal flipping subset. A dimension that pushes the failure rate to ≥95% with the same failure signature is the cause class. It goes into flakes.json as forced_by, and into the brief as a confirmed hypothesis with the forcing command as its probe.
- first slice: `fm determinize "<cmd>" [--runs 10]` with the dimensions that need no shim: 1 CPU, hash-seed sweep, TZ and LANG variants, reversed test order. Plus redacted env snapshots stored per env_id in check_run, and `--env-diff` ddmin between the last passing and the failing run's env. Tests: a toy test that depends on set iteration order (forced by the seed) and one that depends on TZ.
- bar/numbers: 2: flaky and environment-dependent bugs are the long, expensive tail of debugging time. 4: gives the user a deterministic repro of a heisenbug, which nothing offered before.
- verdict: Passes: 'fails 20/20 with PYTHONHASHSEED=3 and reversed order' is a real wait-what moment that no agent offers, and it's self-contained.

### Foreman debugs itself: shrink every guard misfire to its minimal core and replay every hook crash [R1 · Debugging intuition; build; escalated; value 4, M]
Foreman's own failures become one-command reproductions with the cause pinned. A false block or a fuzzer escape is delta-debugged to the smallest command that still misfires, plus the guard line that decided it. Clustering by minimal core does mechanically what was done by hand: 65 saved fuzz escapes grouped into 9 classes. Every hook crash keeps its redacted payload, so `fm replay --crash` re-runs it against the working copy and against HEAD. Today's bare tracebacks become failing fixtures, and 'why did it go idle' becomes a replayable Stop decision.
- mechanism: (a) Guard shrinker: fmguard.findings is a pure function of (tool, input, ctx). `fm replay --shrink "<cmd>"` runs ddmin over shlex tokens, then characters within surviving tokens, keeping the predicate 'still blocked with the same category'. For escapes the predicate is 'still runs the dangerous call unblocked', using test_guard_diff's sandbox. A bash -n parse check stops cores from changing meaning. sys.settrace over fmguard while evaluating the core (offline only, never on the hook path) names the last deciding branch. Escapes whose cores share a normalised shape form one class; the class and its fixture lines (the core plus the nearest variant that is allowed) go to plugin/tests/fixtures. (b) Crash flight recorder: log_error's callers, and the 'working copy of fmguard failed; using the committed guard' fallback, write the redacted payload to state/logs/crashes/<sig>.json (deduped, capped at 50). `fm replay --crash [SIG]` runs it against the working-copy library and against HEAD and says which fails. fm doctor reports 'working copy broken since <time> in <file>'. (c) Stall replay: the Stop hook's decision inputs (drive, paused, needs_user verdict, claims_done verdict, queue) go into a 50-entry ring buffer, so an idle session's last decisions replay through the current code.
- first slice: `fm replay --shrink "<cmd>"` (minimal still-blocked command plus the deciding guard line) and `--shrink-escapes`, which shrinks guard_fuzz_escapes.txt and prints the cores grouped into classes. Plus payload capture in the hook error paths and `fm replay --crash`. Tests: a known false-block fixture shrinks to the expected core; a deliberately broken working-copy guard reproduces from its saved payload.
- bar/numbers: 3: each Foreman misfire becomes a minimal regression fixture automatically, so the guard and hooks improve with every failure. 2: time to root cause on guard false blocks, escapes and hook crashes.
- verdict: escalated to: Night school: every past mistake becomes a permanent test, and Foreman rewrites its own skills, hooks and agents overnight against them. Shrinking guard misfires is one producer of never-again cases. Night school turns every misfire into a permanent test and breeds the repair.

### Symptom atlas: precomputed intuition from bugs planted during idle time [R1 · Debugging intuition; build; escalated; value 4, L]
A senior engineer knows that 'when these two tests fail like that, it's the tokenizer'. Foreman learns this before the first real bug. During idle time it plants small mutations in hot code (flipped comparisons, off-by-one, dropped returns, swapped arguments, removed guards) and records which tests fail and with what signature. The result is a project-specific symptom-to-locus atlas. A real failure is matched against it: 'this failing-test set and error shape is produced by mutants in fmguard._split (7 of the 9 nearest)'. Research has mutation-based fault localisation (Metallaxis, MUSE); no agent precomputes it as standing intuition.
- mechanism: fm night job. It picks hot files from fm map (most-changed, with test↔source links) and generates ≤N AST mutants per function with stdlib ast for Python (Compare flip, BinOp ±1, Return→None, negated If, deleted guard clause), token operators for other languages later. For each mutant it runs only the linked fast tests in a scratch worktree, reusing prove --hunks' worktree and mtime helpers. It stores {locus file/func/line/operator, failing test ids, failure_signature tokens, error class} in state/projects/<slug>/atlas.jsonl, along with the tree it was built on. Online: on a red gate, similarity = Jaccard over failing test sets plus overlap of signature tokens, aggregated per function, and down-weighted for functions changed since the atlas tree (git diff). The ranked loci feed the Suspects board, and the gym scores them. Surviving mutants (no test fails) are noted as untested behaviour, a side effect rather than the goal. It uses idle CPU and no tokens.
- first slice: `fm atlas build --files PATH… --max-mutants 200 --minutes 20` for Python, and `fm atlas match "<gate>"` ranking functions for the current failure. Test: build the atlas on a toy repo, then plant a real (non-atlas) bug. Its function must rank in the top 3.
- bar/numbers: 1: a precomputed, project-specific localisation prior that no agent has. 3: idle time turns into better future diagnoses, which also serves the user's 'never idle' rule without spending tokens.
- verdict: escalated to: Bug nose: names the bug before a file is read, and smells bug-shaped hunks at edit time, graded on every red. Planted bugs are the nose's synthetic training half, not a separate product.

### Hindsight autopsy: when the answer was already on screen, and the habit that would have caught it [R1 · Debugging intuition; build; escalated; value 4, M]
After each long debug episode, Foreman finds the earliest tool output that already contained the eventual fix site, and measures how many calls passed before the agent acted on it. Over many episodes this gives process rules grounded in the user's own history, for example: 'in rustc failures the first error[E…] line named the fix site in 8 of 10 episodes; reading the last summary line cost a median 30 calls'. Those rules feed fm evolve of debugging.md and fm-debugger.md, gated by the gym. No agent measures its own attention misses. The reasoning theme's Trace cards replay the decisive move of one past case; this measures the systematic gap between when information was visible and when it was used.
- mechanism: Input: genome episodes with causal hunks. Locus tokens = the file basename, the names of the functions enclosing the hunks, and distinctive identifiers in the changed lines. Scan the episode's tool results in order. 'Visible at k' is the first result with a file:line match or ≥2 locus tokens in an error context. 'Acted at m' is the first edit to the locus. Gap = m − k. Classify what showed it (traceback frame index, grep hit, a test output section, a file Read) and what the agent did in between (re-runs, edits elsewhere, reads elsewhere). A night job aggregates the results into hindsight.jsonl. fm friction gets a 'missed answers' section with the top recurring miss kinds per language. Each recurring kind becomes a self-inbox item (fm capture --source self) with its episodes as evidence and a draft rule. fm evolve can apply the rule to debugging.md, and the gym plus fm bench decide whether it stays.
- first slice: `fm autopsy --days 30` over registered projects' transcripts. It detects red-to-green episodes of ≥20 calls, takes the locus from the edits that immediately preceded green, computes visible-at and acted-at, and prints the 10 largest gaps with the kind of output that showed the answer, plus a per-kind summary. No LLM, no evolve yet.
- bar/numbers: 3: Foreman's debugging procedure improves from measured misses, with proof. 1: no agent measures when the information it needed first appeared.
- verdict: escalated to: Night school: every past mistake becomes a permanent test, and Foreman rewrites its own skills, hooks and agents overnight against them.
